## Approved keyframes that failed QA

- **1.2-05** (recompose): The actor, period barracks setting, uniform and basic proportions are convincing, but the central dramatic design is missed. The sergeant faces and looks almost directly into the lens rather than appearing in three-quarter profile with a sideways glance, his expression lacks the specified dry almost-smile, and his mouth is illuminated rather than shadowed. The visible bulb is behind him while strong frontal light illuminates his face and uniform, undermining the intended single-bulb lighting.
- **1.2-06** (edit): The staging, identities, barracks setting, period equipment and anatomy are convincing, but the foreground private has a serious, open-mouthed expression rather than the designed small surprised smile. That changes the emotional point of the shot, although it is a localized and readily editable flaw.
- **1.3-03** (face_swap): The setting, costume, prop, performance, lighting, and casting likeness are convincing, but the girl's head is visibly oversized relative to her narrow shoulders. Because this is the lead and the disproportion would persist through animation, the frame is not ready as is.
- **1.4-04b** (recompose): The officer, casting identity, night-tent setting, period look, and off-camera gaze are convincing. However, the framing omits the sealed envelope and the officer's hand resting on it, which are the shot's specified story action and continuity point.
- **1.6-03** (edit): The casting, anatomy, dawn lighting, airfield grove setting and broad 1973 military look are convincing. However, the Malyutka is already fully exposed and mounted on the opened/deployed suitcase launcher, making the specified next action—opening the case and then touching the missile—physically impossible. Small pseudo-lettering is also visible on the sight hood despite the no-text rule.
- **2.11-02** (new_prompt): The counting action, exhausted formation, weapons, night palette and lead casting read well, with no obvious anatomy or compositing failure. However, the designed shallow fold between dunes and illuminated dune crests are entirely absent; the frame instead shows the perfectly flat terrain required by LOC-0004, exposing a contradiction in the shot design. The framing is also wider than the specified medium shot, and the captain's ornate dark rank boards and modern-looking wrist device need period refinement.
- **2.4-02** (edit): The composition, profile performance, taped glasses, radio silhouette and period uniform read well, and the operator's anatomy is natural. However, the red source is substantially brighter and broader than the designed dim filtered glow, while the paper carries clearly visible writing despite the production's no-text rule. The flashlight also has a potentially modern tactical form, though its exact date is uncertain.
- **2.5-03** (edit): The macro insert, salt-crusted hand, period RPG-7 furniture, nocturnal palette and shallow focus are convincing. However, the launcher and fingertips are visibly at or in the brine rather than carried clear above it, breaking the designed action and the production rule for weapons in the marsh.
- **2.5-07** (recompose): The composition, captain performance, cast identity and shallow single-file march are strong, but much of the marsh surface reads as thick floating ice or raised mineral slabs rather than a thin salt crust over black mud. That conspicuous location and material failure would propagate through animation. Some rifle furniture is also too dark and indistinct to confirm the required fixed-stock wooden AKMs.
- **2.6-05** (face_swap): The close-up, casting identity, wet uniform, moonlit night palette and salt-marsh setting are convincing. However, Mostafa looks directly into the lens rather than ahead along his march; this creates an immediately noticeable posed-portrait effect and violates the anatomy/gaze requirement for the designed action.
- **2.7-04** (recompose): The nocturnal Cairo-flat atmosphere and C-13 casting are convincing, but the frame misses the shot's central action: she is holding the portrait away from the wall with both hands rather than reaching up to straighten an already-hung, already-straight frame. The composition is also wider than the specified medium shot, the thimble is absent, and the soldier's head is oversized relative to the shoulders within the portrait.
- **3.10-01** (recompose): The period alley, four-boy count, lighting, casting match and general composition are convincing, but the designed action is not present: only one boy is crawling as a tank while C-14 kneels prominently with the airplane. C-14 also looks directly into the lens rather than participating in the game. The focal toy has a somewhat generated, indistinct airplane shape.
- **3.2-01** (edit): The setting, period atmosphere, radio-focused action, lead placement and casting are strong and photorealistic. However, nine village men are visible rather than the designed eight, making the crowded group composition incorrect.
- **3.2-02** (recompose): The girl, radio, shop setting, daylight and facial integration are convincing, and C-12 matches the casting portrait. However, the prominent village men wear collared shirts and trousers rather than the specifically designed galabeyas, and the girl's required two long braids are not clearly depicted. These visible costume and character-design misses make the frame unsuitable as the animation start frame without recomposition.
- **3.4-05** (new_prompt): The composition strongly conveys a close-range RPG ambush, with credible daylight, scale and period texture. However, C-03's intended profile, eye-to-sight contact and recognizable cast face are hidden, and the RPG's optical sight arrangement does not resemble a correctly mounted PGO-7. The Patton also conflicts with the production rule specifying Sho't Kal Centurions for the Israeli armored force.
- **3.7-01** (recompose): The actor, desert lighting, period clothing and overall missile setup are convincing, but the frame misses the shot's central action: his eye is visibly separated from a sight that lacks a clear rubber eyecup, and the hand/control interface is not clearly the required small joystick. The image is also 3:2 rather than full-frame 16:9. These flaws affect the first-frame performance and require recomposition before animation.
- **4.1-03** (edit): The casting, anatomy, period look, desert setting and shouting head turn are convincing. However, the AKM butt is held across the upper chest rather than seated in the right shoulder pocket, directly breaking the designed shouldered firing posture and making the next action difficult to animate credibly.
- **4.5-03** (recompose): The composition, action, smoke, lead casting and broad 1973 desert-war look are strong, but the shot-defining RKG-3 grenades are visibly the wrong shape and color, several runners do not carry them as designed, C-06 lacks recognizable Malyutka equipment, and one commando violates the bare-headed rule.
- **4.9-06** (edit): Strong photorealistic character frame with natural anatomy, convincing identity, and appropriate desert setting, but the fire geography contradicts the shot design: burning tanks are visibly behind the operator even though they should be behind the camera, and the required orange reflections are absent from his glasses. The clipped radio handset also looks potentially too modern.
- **5.4-05** (recompose): The action, location, lead identity, night lighting and physical staging are convincing, but only four of the specified seven seated survivors are visible and the captain's prominent shoulder insignia is historically wrong. It shows a single pale asterisk-like device rather than three gold five-pointed captain's stars.
- **5.5-02** (recompose): The village, period atmosphere, jar, lighting and character placement are convincing, but the shot is framed as a full-body long shot rather than the designed medium shot. The girl is standing with both feet planted instead of being caught mid-stride, and her dress ends around mid-calf rather than at the ankle. These visible design mismatches should be corrected before animation.
- **5.5-03** (recompose): The setting, casting likeness, period look, oven, clothing and daylight are convincing, but the central story action is not staged as designed. The mother appears fully upright beside the oven rather than half-risen from kneeling, weakening the intended moment before she slowly completes her rise.
- **5.6-05** (face_swap): The composition, performance, casting identity, period clothing, blue-blackout window and letter-reading action are convincing. However, C-13's head is visibly oversized relative to her narrow shoulder span, which is a major anatomy issue in this tight first frame and should be corrected before animation.
- **T.2-01** (edit): The wide, low rear-cabin composition, troop arrangement and period atmosphere are convincing, with generally sound anatomy and integrated faces. The central anti-tank payload is visibly wrong: the supposed Malyutka is an oversized bomb-like missile rather than a small 9M14 missile on its fibreglass suitcase launcher, and the required mine sacks are not clearly represented. This prominent aisle prop would remain a major continuity and historical error throughout the push-in.
